Platts Oilgram Price Report
Volume 104 / Issue 195 / October 12, 2026

News
European diesel market doubts Trump's Russian import plan will ease tightness
Traders see promised volumes as relatively small Gasoil spreads drop but price disruption risk
Hurricane, Middle East events sway diesel more: market sources
European diesel market participants expect little relief to the global diesel balance from US President Donald Trump's plan to import Russian diesel, doubting it will ease tightness or weigh on prices.
Trump said Oct. 9 that Russia would supply additional diesel to the market.
"I have just concluded a highly successful discussion with President Vladimir Putin of Russia, wherein it was agreed that Russia will immediately supply over 300,000 Tons of Diesel Fuel to the American and Global Marketplace, another 500,000 Tons during the month of November, and 1,000,000 Tons immediately thereafter," Trump said on his Truth Social media account.
"Additionally, based on the condition of their Diesel Refineries, Russia will then deliver, within a short period of time, 3,000,000 Tons of Diesel Fuel," Trump said.
A European diesel trader said: "The flat price did respond a little; however, it is a relatively small quantity versus what Russia used to export, and it largely comes without a timeline, so the market is lacking clarity."
Another Europe-based market source said: "We do not see it moving anything materially. It is just a readjustment of flows ... Russia will not have a surplus to export."

Market analysis
International Crude
OPEC+ crude production increases in September, driven by Saudi rebound
Output rises 1.61 million b/d on month to wartime high Countries with quotas 5.17 million b/d below target
OPEC+ crude output rose by 1.61 million barrels/day month over month in September, led by a recovery in Saudi Arabia, the latest Platts OPEC+ survey from S&P Global Energy found.
OPEC+ produced 35.27 million b/d in September, well below levels of 42.89 million b/d in February, before the US and Israel attacked Iran, triggering attacks on energy infrastructure and severe shipping restrictions in the region. The output fall is also partially due to the UAE leaving OPEC in May.
September output was at a wartime high, as some producers in the Middle East exported more crude.

(continued on page 15)

OPEC output rose by 1.58 million b/d month over month to 21.24 million b/d, led by a recovery in Saudi Arabia, which produced 8 million b/d, up 1.65 million b/d month over month, the survey showed.
Iraq also contributed to the rise, boosting production by 200,000 b/d to 3.9 million b/d. This was due to a 25% increase in exports, as more Iraqi oil passed through the Strait of Hormuz and Ceyhan on Turkey's Mediterranean coast.
Non-OPEC allies increased production by 20,000 b/d to 14.03 million b/d in September, as Russia managed to hold output steady after months of cuts linked to multiple attacks on its refining infrastructure.

(continued on page 3)

Despite the output increase, OPEC+ members with quotas continue to produce well below target. Production was 5.17 million b/d below quota in September, the survey showed.
Amid lower production volumes, a group of seven producers implementing voluntary cuts kept quotas at August levels in September.
The voluntary cutters, which include Saudi Arabia, Russia, Iraq, Kuwait, Kazakhstan, Algeria and Oman, plan to keep quotas at September levels in October and November, OPEC said previously.
The voluntary cutters next plan to meet on Nov. 1. A full ministerial meeting as well as a meeting of the joint ministerial monitoring committee that oversees the agreement are scheduled for Nov. 29, OPEC said previously.
OPEC+ has benefited from higher prices during the war. Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed Dated Brent at a wartime high of $144.42/barrel on April 7. Prices have been volatile since then and Dated Brent was last assessed at $132.585/b on Oct. 9.

Asia's heavy sour crude shortage lifts Canadian Cold Lake, AWB Pacific premiums
Cold Lake CFR Asia premium hits record high in Sept
South Korea triples imports from Canada
Disruptions hit Middle East heavy sour crude flows
Spot premiums for Cold Lake and Access Western Blend crude oil delivered to East Asia via the Pacific have been trending higher as major regional refiners place greater value on the Canadian heavy sour grades amid disruptions to heavy sour Middle Eastern supplies, according to North Asian refinery sources and South Korean foreign affairs and trade ministries.
Although refiners have secured ample non-Middle Eastern crude since April as part of supply-diversification efforts, alternative sources of heavy sour crude remain limited. As a result, Canadian Cold Lake and Access Western Blend grades are gaining popularity among South Korean, Japanese and Chinese refiners, according to feedstock and trading managers based in Ulsan, Chiba, Beijing, Yeosu and Negishi who spoke with Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, during market discussions over Oct. 6-12.
Platts assessed Cold Lake at a record-high premium of $9.5/b against front-month Dubai on CFR South Korea basis Sept. 21. The premium averaged $4.33/b to date in October, compared to September's average premium of $3.67/b and the first half's average of a 5-cent/b discount.

Canadian crude imports in the first eight months reached 10.9 million barrels, close to a threefold rise from 3.9 million barrels purchased during the same period a year earlier, latest data from state-run Korea National Oil Corp. issued Sept. 25 showed.
South Korea and Canada agreed to deepen energy cooperation as Korean imports of Canadian crude oil and LNG continue to rise, South Korea's Ministry of Foreign Affairs said in a statement Oct. 9.

Access Western Blend, strengthening Canada's position as an alternative supplier amid Middle Eastern flow disruptions, South Korea's Ministry of Trade, Industry and Resources said in its second half 2026 industry briefing.
Meanwhile, China imported 1.41 million metric tons, or around 10.34 million barrels, of crude oil from Canada in August, up 34.9% from a year earlier and 13% from the previous month, according to data from China's General Administration of Customs.
During January-August, China's crude imports from Canada rose 14.9% year over year to 11.14 million mt, or about 81.66 million barrels, the data showed.

South Korean Foreign Minister Cho Hyun and Canadian Foreign Minister Anita Anand agreed to strengthen cooperation on stable energy supplies and trade flows, highlighting major Canadian infrastructure initiatives such as the Pacific Link western oil pipeline project, according to MOFA.
Additional western Canadian pipeline and export capacity would improve Pacific access for grades such as Cold Lake and

Elsewhere, Japan received a rare cargo of Cold Lake crude on Aug. 10 to make up for difficulty in securing heavy sour Middle Eastern term and spot barrels, a feedstock manager at a major Japanese refiner based in Negishi told Platts, indicating that some quick spot deals had been concluded during Alberta Premier Danielle Smith's April visit to Japan.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 12, 2026

Alberta Premier Danielle Smith visited Japan back in April to meet various crude importers and state-run energy companies, according to an Alberta government statement issued April 21.
Heavy sour crudes East Asian refiners have been able to secure adequate
volumes of light and medium sour Middle Eastern grades through ports that bypass the Strait of Hormuz. However, Basrah Medium, Arab Medium and Arab Heavy have been more difficult to procure because their loading ports and terminals are located deep inside the Gulf, according to the feedstock and trading managers at Northeast Asian refiners.
Japan saw 14 million barrels of light sour Murban crude arrive in August, but the country did not receive any Arab Medium and Arab Heavy crudes in the same month, the latest data from the Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry issued Sept. 30 showed.
Asia's third-biggest crude importer, South Korea, also secured more light and medium sour Abu Dhabi grades so far this year as crude imports from the UAE over January-August rose 11.4% year over year to 101.6 million barrels, KNOC data showed.
However, Saudi crude shipments during the first eight months fell 15.6% year over year to 196.2 million barrels due to a shortfall in Arab Medium and Arab Heavy procurement, according to the KNOC data and the Ulsan-based refinery feedstock manager.
China's total crude imports over January-August fell 14.6% year over year to 9.68 million b/d as heavy sour Middle Eastern grades such as Arab Heavy face challenges entering the Chinese market, according to a Shandong refinery source referencing the latest GAC data.
-- Gawoon Vahn, Daisy Xu - Oil Market Specialist, Takeo Kumagai
Nigeria eyes 788,000 b/d oil output boost from shut-in wells: official
77 wells already re-entered so far this year Local firms focused on well re-entries after IOC exits New bid round offers 40 blocks for development
Nigeria's upstream regulator said late Oct. 11 that it has set its sights on restoring around 788,000 barrels/day of shut-in oil

production as part of a broader push to reach its output target of 3 million b/d by 2030.
The chief executive of the Nigerian Upstream Petroleum Regulatory Commission, Oritsemeyiwa Eyesan, said that between January and September at least 77 oil wells had been successfully re-entered, adding that the commission has sanctioned drilling programs for 128 wells.
Nigeria has struggled to meet its production targets in recent years, with field maturation, theft and vandalism, and underinvestment, particularly in exploration, eroding output.
Many oil wells have been abandoned by producers due to high operating and security costs, compounded by the recent onshore divestments by international oil majors.
However, local producers, including Seplat Energy and the Renaissance consortium, which purchased onshore and shallowwater assets from ExxonMobil and Shell respectively, have set about re-entering shut-in wells.
"Our key priority is simple - restoring the more than 788,000 barrels per day of shut-in production identified across 63 operators," Eyesan was quoted as saying. "This is besides the licensing rounds and accelerating [contract] approval processes."
The NUPRC on Oct. 7 launched a fresh licensing round with 40 oil and gas blocks on offer, which it hopes will eventually add 1.74 million b/d to Nigeria's oil production capacity and 13.9 billion cubic feet/day to its gas capacity, once its fields are fully developed.
Nigeria's oil production, including condensates, averaged 1.7 million b/d in August, according to government estimates.
-- Newsdesk-Nigeria
Saudi Arabia plans 1.2 mil b/d oil pipeline boost with gas line retrofit: minister
Says 1.2 million b/d of capacity within 7-8 weeks
Total Saudi pipeline capacity to near 10 mil b/d within 2-3 years
US rejoins International Energy Forum
Saudi Arabia plans to temporarily convert a gas pipeline to transport additional oil to help address disruptions to its export capacity due to the conflict in the Middle East, energy minister Prince Abdulaziz bin Salman said Oct. 12.
Bin Salman said that an unspecified gas pipeline could be retrofitted with pumping stations and compressors to accommodate oil flows and provide an estimated 1.2 million b/d of capacity within seven to eight weeks.
The pipeline would be lent to the Saudi Arabian Oil Co. until "May", then returned for use as a gas pipeline ahead of the peakdemand summer season, he said without specifying a year while speaking at the 25th WPC Energy Congress in Riyadh.
"That pipeline, with some more investment, we will take it up to 3 million [barrels per day]," bin Salman said. Alongside a planned improvement to the East-West Pipeline's pumping stations, Saudi Arabia could reach 10 million b/d in pipeline transportation flows within two to three years, he added.

QatarEnergy cut the November official selling price differentials for its Qatar Land and Marine crude grades by $3/b month over month, according to a company notice posted on its website.

Chadian crude grade Doba saw a cargo offered in the Platts Market on Close assessment process on Oct. 12 for the first time since November 2025 as freight costs continued to pressure free-on-board differentials in the West African market.
Glencore offered a cargo of the grade in the day's session, scheduled to load across Nov. 10-11, down from a $13.25/barrel discount to Dated Brent to a $16.20/b discount, which was left outstanding at the day's close without a buyer.
Platts assessed Doba at a $16.25/b discount on Oct. 12, down by $2.95/b day on day, and representing the grade's all-time lowest assessment since it was launched by Platts in August 2015.
"Buyers are still stepping back," one Europe-based WAF crude trader told Platts on the day, pointing to still elevated shipping costs as a hindrance to sentiment. "Suppliers must lower their indications or they will have all their cargoes with them until the next cycle."
The last time Doba was offered in the MOC was on Nov. 28, 2025, when Vitol offered a December 2025 loading clip down to a $2.45/b discount to Dated Brent, which too was left outstanding at that day's London close.

Shell ramping up shut-in Gulf of Mexico oil production
Output started up from five assets
Personnel being redeployed to sites
604,503 b/d crude output still shut in
Shell is in the process of ramping up crude oil production from its Gulf of Mexico assets that were shut in during Hurricane Isaias, company spokesperson Cindy Babski said Oct. 12.
The re-deployment of personnel to the assets that were shut in has now been completed, Babski said.
On Oct. 7, Shell said it was shutting in production as a precautionary measure at its Mars, Olympus, Ursa, Vito, and Appomattox assets.
"There is no storm damage to our Mars, Olympus, Ursa, Vito, and Appomattox assets, and we are continuing the process of redeployment," the company said in its Oct. 10 update without indicating the volume of crude oil that was shut in.
At its last count on Oct. 9, the Marine Minerals Administration said a total of 1.459 million barrels/day of oil equivalent oil output and 1.26 billion cubic feet/day of natural gas production was shut in response to the hurricane.
Hurricane Isaias made landfall near Destin, Florida, around 8.30 pm CDT Oct. 9 with maximum sustained winds of 105 miles/ hour, according to the National Hurricane Center.
30% crude output still shut in Meanwhile, some 30% or a total of 604,503 b/d of crude oil
and 25% of natural gas or 531 MMcf/d of natural gas production

still remains shut in, MMA said in its latest update at 1 pm CDT on Oct. 12.
Besides Shell, Chevron, and BP had also curtailed production. While no comments were immediately available from Chevron, BP Oct. 10 it was returning personnel and working to safely restart production at its Na Kika and Thunder Horse platforms. "We also are returning non-essential personnel to our Argos, Atlantis and Mad Dog platforms," BP said then.

Petroecuador lifts force majeure on SOTE oil pipeline shipments
SOTE pipeline operating at 312,000 b/d
Firm added sixth a pumping unit
Firm held Tres Bocas-Pascuales fuel pipeline bid round
Petroecuador lifted its force majeure on crude shipments after ramping up operations at its 360,000 b/d SOTE oil pipeline, the company said Oct. 10.
The pipeline, halted for four days at the start of the month following to a fire, is currently operating at 312,000 b/d after adding a sixth pumping unit, the state oil company said.
The 498 km SOTE conduit, which links Lago Agrio with its 110,000 b/d Esmeraldas oil refinery on the northwestern Pacific coast, was halted Oct. 1 due to a fire at a pumping station and reopened Oct. 5, followed by a gradual ramp-up process.
The SOTE fire came after a slew of incidents at Petroecuador's aging installations, including shutdowns of the Esmeraldas refinery, the main processing plant at its Block 43 ITT oilfields and the 74,400 b/d Esmeraldas-Santo Domingo fuel pipeline earlier this year.
At the same time, Petroecuador Oct. 12 held a bid round to upgrade a 20.6 km stretch of its 96,000 b/d Tres Bocas-Pascuales fuel pipeline.
The $19.8 million, 13-month contract drew offers from seven bidders, according to the state company's website.
No date has been set as yet to award the winning bid. Tres Bocas fuel terminal in Guayaquil, which distributes 70% of the country's gasoline, diesel and LPG deliveries, handled 14.23 million barrrels of refined fuels through July, according to Petroecuador.

Platts European Jet Daily Market Analysis
US-Europe arbitrage opens at $24.59/barrel Oct.8: CERA
China lifts export ban Oct. 8, easing future supply tightness
European jet differentials surged Oct. 12 as the steep backwardation in the underlying ICE low sulfur gasoil contract came to fruition at the expiry of the October contract. Flat prices, however, remained relatively unchanged.
The underlying market appeared less tight than previously expected, with one offer in the Platts Market on Close assessment process that was left untraded by the close.
Although strong freight rates have continued to provide a floor for jet fuel prices delivered into Europe, they have fallen in recent days, opening up arbitrage opportunities from the US Gulf Coast and East Asia. Jet fuel into Europe is assessed as a CIF price, factoring in the cost of freight. Arbitrage from the US was wide open, according to data from S&P Global Energy CERA, which recorded the incentive to bring jet from the US to Northwest Europe at $24.59/barrel on Oct.8.
In the US, the return of Delta Air Lines Trainer refinery was set to pull back some strength from soaring jet fuel costs on the US Atlantic Coast over the coming quarter. During its thirdquarter results call, CFO Erik Snell said the refinery was fully back online after the temporary outage discussed in July, positioning

October ICE low sulfur gasoil futures physical delivery totals 439 lots
Delivery drops 40.7% from September expiry
New Mo1 contract assessed at $1,410.75/mt
Physical delivery of the October Intercontinental Exchange low sulfur gasoil futures contract, which expired at midday London time Oct. 12 totaled 439 lots, or 43,900 metric tons, according to ICE data, down 40.7% from the 740 lots, or 74,000 mt, delivered against the September contract.
Exxon, Trafigura and Vitol were taking in the October contract expiry, while Shell and TOTSA were giving, according to market participants.
Exxon and Trafigura reportedly took 10,000 mt and 3,000 mt, respectively, while Vitol received 24,000 mt, according to the same sources. Exxon confirmed its quantity, but the other companies were not immediately available for comment.
Meanwhile, Shell delivered 30,000 mt and TOTSA, 14,400 mt. Neither company was available for comment.
The September ICE LSGO contract expired at $1,425.50/ mt, with the September-October spread settling at $35/mt in backwardation, ICE data showed.
By comparison, the September contract expired at $1,472.75/ mt, $47.25/mt above the October contract.
Gasoil futures had a notable sell-off at the beginning of October, following the G7's commitment to release up to 100 million barrels of strategic oil reserves within four months, and the ruling out of a potential US diesel export ban. Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the October ICE LSGO futures contract down $95.75/mt to $1,355.50/mt on Oct. 2, the lowest level since Aug. 28.
Prices came under further pressure over the Oct. 10- 11 weekend, after US President Donald Trump announced his plan to import Russian diesel and separately declared that an energy ceasefire between Russia and Ukraine was "effective immediately."

"The price action on gasoil spreads was an initial sell-off," said one European diesel market source.
Platts assessed the new November front month at $1,410.75/ mt at 1630 GMT on Oct. 12, below the price at which the October contract expired, and down $78.75/mt day over day.
ICE LSGO futures are a physically deliverable futures contract for barges of open-origin 10 parts per million ultra-low sulfur diesel within the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp hub.
-- Sophia Aung
Russia partially lifts diesel export ban
Exports of 500,000 mt/month approved
Analysts see limited market impact
The Russian government has partially lifted a diesel export ban, after US President Donald Trump said that Russia had agreed to supply global markets amid concerns over supply shortages and high prices.
Analysts said that Russian refiners are able to supply some volumes to global markets, but are unlikely to cover the supply deficit caused by interruptions to exports from the Middle East.
The Russian government said that the export ban was partially lifted from Oct. 10 and at this stage allows for the export of 500,000 mt of diesel.
"Russian oil companies, in coordination with the government, will immediately begin contracting diesel supplies with foreign counterparties," a government statement said.
Russian Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak said that Russia is ready to supply 300,000 mt of diesel to the US market and other partners in October, according to the Tass news agency.
He confirmed Trump's estimates that Russia will supply up to 500,000 mt in November and said that supplies could rise to 1 million mt in December, the report said.
"Going forward, the volume could rise to 3 million mt/month. At the same time, the Russian market will also be fully supplied with diesel," Novak said.
Independent energy analyst George Voloshin said that supplying up to 500,000 mt/month is operationally feasible using existing inventories and seasonal refinery turnarounds, but scaling up to 1-3 million mt/month would face severe physical bottlenecks.
"Sustained Ukrainian drone strikes on secondary refining and hydrocracking units across western Russia have constrained domestic production of high-grade winter diesel. Forcing multimillion-ton monthly exports without fully repaired processing units risks creating domestic supply crunches and sharp wholesale price spikes inside Russia," he said.
S&P Global Energy CERA analysts said that they see the market impact of the deal as limited, in a note released Oct. 11.
"[Global] diesel exports are down by over 1 million b/d largely due to refinery outages in Russia and the Middle East. These Russian volumes, if truly incremental, would be only 10% of that global total," they said.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

9

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 12, 2026

They see a lasting energy ceasefire between Russia and Ukraine as more consequential, if it comes into force.
Domestic supplies Diesel supply has recently been sufficient to cover both retail
and spot market demand inside Russia, according to Russiabased market sources. Market participants said that in contrast to gasoline, diesel is available at all retail stations. The farming sector has also been provided with the necessary volumes during the autumn agricultural campaign, which is now drawing to a close.
Market participants had said previously that the diesel ban would need to be lifted as refineries would otherwise struggle to place the summer diesel production, which is no longer needed domestically as the country switches to intermediate and winter grades.
Russia imposed a diesel export ban on so-called nonproducers, which includes traders and small refineries with up to 1 million mt/year processing capacity, at the end of January 2026. This ban was set to be in force until the end of January 2027. On July 9, the diesel export ban was also applied to large refineries and was planned to be in effect until the end of October.
Voloshin said that the deal is unlikely to impact Russian crude output, which has fallen in recent months, partially due to strikes on refineries.
"Russian refiners may run primary distillation units harder at the expense of product quality and yield, or, if domestic downstream capacity cannot absorb more runs, Russia will simply continue exporting unrefined crude east to Asian buyers while keeping overall wellhead production steady," he said.
Russian crude output has fallen in 2026 according to the Platts OPEC+ crude production survey. It produced 8.8 million b/d in August, down from 9.23 million b/d in January.

European paper Hi-lo spread narrows to four-month low
Spread narrows $10.25/mt on day Oct. 9
LSFO blending demand strengthens in ARA
The front-month European Hi-lo spread -- the differential between FOB NWE 1% sulfur fuel oil cargo and 3.5% sulfur fuel oil barges swaps in Rotterdam -- narrowed to a four-month low on Oct. 9.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the front-month Hi-lo spread at a four-month low of minus $2.50/metric ton Oct. 9, narrowing $10.25/mt day over day. The spread was last seen lower on June 10.
All prompt month Hi-lo spreads narrowed day over day. Despite the negative Hi-Lo spread, market participants have indicated firmer fundamentals within the Northwest European LSFO market in recent days. "1% is stronger again," said a Northwest European trader

who indicated increased blending demand in the AmsterdamRotterdam-Antwerp region.
Overall, however, market participants anticipate softer 1% demand in the cooler winter months in the Mediterranean, which could put pressure on the complex.
In the high sulfur fuel oil market, demand remained limited, with traders citing Brent futures volatility and fluctuating ZRE-A ticket prices in Rotterdam as key headwinds.
ARA fuel oil stocks fell 4.5% week over week to 918,000 mt in the seven days to Oct. 8, reversing the prior week's build, according to Insights Global.
In the Mediterranean, by contrast, traders reported demand picking up in the West Med for midmonth.

Naphtha East-West spread hits over six-month high as clean freight soars
Mediterranean cargoes shift to Asia
European supply tightens, bullish outlook
The naphtha east-west spread has reached its highest level in over six months on the back of soaring freight rates as tensions in the Middle East
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the front-month East-West spread - the difference between the naphtha CFR MOPJ and the Naphtha CIF NWE cargo - at $86/mt on Oct. 9, the highest since March 31, when it was at $100.50/mt.
The latest move comes amid continued strength in Asian naphtha markets and elevated freight costs, which have boosted delivered values into the region and strengthened arbitrage economics.
"Hearing east freight is very strong, so owners want to position vessels there," a trading source said.
Indeed, freight rates for naphtha shipments from the Gulf to North Asia have climbed to record highs, driven by a shortage of available LR2 tonnage after several vessels moved into the dirty products market this year to capitalize on stronger returns.
Platts assessed the PG-Japan L2 rate at $225.430/mt Oct. 12. Rates have been rising steadily from June 2 when rates were assessed at $54.10/mt.
A wider East-West spread typically incentivizes the movement of Atlantic Basin cargoes into Asia, which market participants said has already started happening.
"Some of the Mediterranean naphtha cargoes [are] heading east, so overall Europe looks tighter," the trading source added.
As Mediterranean barrels are drawn eastward, availability in Europe declines, tightening regional balances. This is evident in the steeper backwardation and stronger cracks.
"Looks like October should stay supported, and let's see if this Asian pull rolls forward," the trading source added.
Another indication of the strong market was the physical premium to swaps price. Platts assessed the physical CIF NWE

Platts US LPG Spot Daily Commentary
Propane surges past $1/gal in Gulf Coast
Inventories could be peaking early, analysts say
Butane-propane spread hits widest in 11 years

Propane in the US Gulf Coast reached over $1/gal for the first time in over four years, Oct. 12, though butane prices continued to outpace propane.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed propane up 3.25 cents to $1.0075 cent/gal, the highest since Sept. 19, 2022, according to Platts data. Energy Transfer propane increased its premium to Enterprise, up to 3.75 cents, the widest since Aug. 31.
Gulf Coast propane has continued to rise sharply since mid-September, and this comes as US propane and propylene inventories may already have reached their 2026 peak, according to S&P Global Energy CERA analysts.
"This is significant because the 2026 inventory peak was observed in September, earlier than the typical seasonal high in late October," CERA analysts said in their Oct. 12 propane market forecast. "As a result, the 2026 and 2025 weekly inventory trends are likely to converge soon."
Even so, the Enterprise butane-Energy Transfer propane spread increased to 70.5 cents on the day, the widest since February 2013.
Enterprise butane increased 11 cents to $1.75 cents/gal, the highest since March 2022, signifying the ongoing reaction to the subpar inventory trend and robust demand from both waterborne exports and domestic gasoline blending demand.
-- Hunter Marrow

US disables ship bound for China with iron ore
Middle East ship attacks surge in October: UKMTO
The US attacked the stern of Panama-flagged cargo ship Ocean Molica (also known as Akira Sun) in the Gulf of Oman outside the Strait of Hormuz after it tried to sail through the US blockade on Oct. 10 in alleged violation of any transits entering or departing an Iranian port, the US Central Command said on X Oct. 11.
Akira Sun was headed for China's Lanshan port, carrying iron ore, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea. Western Bulk Management of Norway is listed as the manager and it did not respond to a request for comment on Oct. 11
The US blockade on Iranian shipping resumed July 14, with four commercial ships disabled and 135 ships turned around since then, CENTCOM said. "Additionally, US forces have destroyed 10 tankers linked to the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps' multibillion-dollar shadow network over the same period," CENTCOM said.
Ship attacks around the Strait of Hormuz between Oman and Iran have risen this month, with the latest incident occurring Oct. 10 at 1920 GMT, when the master of an outbound tanker reported a port-side attack by an unknown projectile, resulting in a fire on board, the UK Maritime Trade Organization said on X Oct. 11. It was the 13th ship attack reported this month by UKMTO.

Platts East of Suez Clean Tanker Daily Commentary
LR2 freight at record high
MRs hold steady
The Long Range, or LR2 freight, was sharply higher, setting a new all-time record Oct. 12 and even flipped to command a premium over the LR1s in Worldscale points due to the limited supply of this vessel size amid an increase in switch to the dirty segment, market participants said.
The clean tankers' freight is "pretty high," but it is still cheaper than the dirty tankers' segment, said a broker in Singapore.
The LR2 supply is so tight that it makes sense for the LR1s to enjoy a discount in Worldscale points on the benchmark Persian Gulf and Oman to North Asia routes, the broker said.
Another broker in Copenhagen expressed similar sentiment. If condensate loading freight in Australia is anything to go by, the LR2 freight in the Middle East will be astronomically higher in uncharted territory, said a chartering executive with a global commodities trading company.
The executive was referring to the FPMC P Ideal, which was placed on subjects by Inpex at around w750 for the Oct. 30 condensate loading on the Ichthys-Singapore route.
The jump in LR2 freight does not necessarily mean that the LR1s have risen by the same proportion or by the same number of points, market participants said.
The LR1 freight is "all over the place," as there still seem to be owners willing to do fixtures around the last done levels in Oman, said the same chartering executive. Unlike the LR2s, which are the main vehicles for transporting refined products to Europe, LR1s on this route are used very little due to economies of scale. This partly explains the exponential increase in LR2 freight on the Middle East-Europe routes.
The STI Stability was placed on subjects at $14 million by ATC for mid-October diesel loading on the Yanbu-UKC route, sources said. The rising freight has provided hardly any solace to charterers such as KPCT that placed the STI Solace on subjects at a historic high of around $16.5 million for Oct. 18 diesel loading via STS on the Sohar-UKC route, sources added.
It will be misleading to compare the earnings of westbound voyages from the Middle East with those of naphtha-loaded cargoes on the Persian Gulf-East routes, said another broker. Tankers that travel westward often have to ballast back in search of cargoes, the broker said.
In the MRs, the rates are stable-to-firm as market participants await the utilization of recently announced export quotas by China, one of the brokers said.
-- Sameer Mohindru

Platts Americas Dirty Tanker Daily Commentary
VLCC Brazil-China run tests w7 lower
Suezmax transatlantic rates climb w5
Aframax local routes sink w50 amid testing
Freight for the Americas VLCC segment started the week on a slightly more bearish tone Oct. 12 amid muted activity, with fresh testing seen solely out of Brazil.
Petrobras booked the Maran Artemis for a Nov. 11-12 loading Brazil-East run at w718, with a $718,000/day demurrage. Discharge options were also negotiated to East Coast India at w758, West Coast India at w783, and the UK Continent/ Mediterranean at w963.
Platts assessed the 260,000 metric ton Brazil-China route down w7 to w718, in line with the latest trade and subsequent market indications.
Following the softer tone set by Petrobras, market indications for the VLCC US Gulf Coast-China run ticked lower amid the lack of fresh testing. The majority of calls were heard between $79 million and $81 million.
Platts assessed the benchmark 270,000 mt USGC-China route down $1 million to $80 million.
For the Suezmax segment, despite a lack of fixtures, calls pushed higher with market participants pointing toward tight tonnage availability.
"Lists are still so short," one shipbroker said, adding that a number of deals had gotten "done quietly," putting upward pressure on rates.
With the bulk of calls heard in the range of w850-w875, Platts assessed the 145,000 mt USGC-UKC run up w5 to w860.
Freight for the benchmark 130,000 mt Guyana-UKC route also finished the day w5 higher at w980.
Aframax rates showed mixed trends on Oct. 12, with fresh activity seen on transatlantic and local runs.
The latest deal saw Eni book a to-be-nominated ship for an Oct. 18-19 loading East Coast Mexico-USGC run at w1,000. Active market indications shifted to the achieved level following the deal's reporting.
Platts assessed the 70,000 mt EC Mexico-USGC route at w1,000, down w50 day over day, leaving a w10 spread with the 70,000 mt Caribbean-USGC run, which was assessed at w990.
On the transatlantic route, one deal was reported after the close of the Oct. 9 Platts Market on Close assessment process, in which Vitol booked the Seafaith loading on Oct. 31 for a USGCIsrael run at w1,300, with a UKCM discharge option at w1,150. Most market participants, however, deemed the trade as not reflective of the market, with the intended destination reported as Israel. The bulk of active market calls were heard at the w950 level for UKC discharge.
Platts assessed the benchmark 70,000 mt USGC-UKC run at w950, up w5.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Catherine Rogers, Paulina Canedo

News
Saudi Aramco CEO says global commercial oil stocks `barely a cushion at all'
Diesel close to $200/b: Nasser at WPC Refilling inventories could take two years Hormuz disruptions threaten energy security

More than 90% of the materials used to restore operations have been sourced from Saudi Arabia, "so we can often do so in hours and days, not weeks and months," Nasser said.

Global commercial onshore oil inventories at less than 6 billion barrels are "barely a cushion at all" against the unforeseen, and "if that is not scary enough, the vast majority of these barrels are locked up," Saudi Aramco CEO Amin Nasser told the WPC Energy Congress in Riyadh Oct. 12.
Inventories "can never be a permanent solution to energy security," Nasser said. "Globally, the squeeze on crude oil is serious. Making the challenge even worse is the spike in refined fuel prices," he said, adding that diesel is around $200/barrel. Refilling inventories while meeting demand could take up to two years, Nasser said during the speech, according to a statement on Aramco's website.
Shipping disruptions and damaged pipelines and plants due to the Iran war since the end of February have upended norms for producers and consumers in the Middle East and beyond.
"Until Hormuz fully reopens and confidence returns, the pressure across the energy chain will only intensify," Nasser said.
Saudi Arabia itself cannot safeguard its energy and shipping deliveries on its own and must involve all nations to protect its infrastructure and routes, Nasser said. Disruption to trade in Hormuz and for other energy infrastructure has "tested every link" in the chain from energy production to consumption, he said.
"When energy infrastructure in this region is targeted, the consequences reach economies everywhere," Nasser said. "The kingdom has invested deeply in protecting these supplies. But no nation, even one as capable and confident as ours, can safeguard them alone."
Master Gas Saudi Aramco's Master Gas System has operated "reliably"
through the Iran war, connecting energy resources with electricity generation, desalination, refining, petrochemicals, and industry, Nasser said. Saudi Arabia is targeting roughly an 80% increase in sales/gas production capacity by 2030 versus 2021 production levels, according to Nasser.
"We are also exploring future opportunities in hydrogen" alongside carbon capture, utilization, and storage and "circular carbon technologies," Nasser said.
The country is also building solar and wind power capacity to diversify electricity supplies and help to lower emissions, he said. Gas and renewables will provide the country's electricity in equal shares by 2030, according to the statement.
The company's refineries are also providing gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, asphalt, and industrial feedstocks to the domestic economy, while petrochemicals are supporting Saudi Arabia's manufacturing, he said.

-- Claudia Carpenter
Hormuz traffic drops to two-week low after ship attacks, US action
Traffic falls to 17 ships from 19 Oct. 10
New wave of maritime attacks around Hormuz
US disables cargo ship violating Iran embargo
Strait of Hormuz traffic dropped to 17 ships Oct. 11, the lowest level in two weeks, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in an Oct. 12 report, following at least four ship attacks near or within the chokepoint since Oct. 10 and a US Central Command strike that disabled a cargo ship violating embargo restrictions on Iranian ports.
The Oct. 11 total was down from 19 ships Oct. 10. Traffic on the Bab al-Mandab Strait dropped to 36 from 38 over the same period, according to the CAS report.
Besides the confirmed ship attacks, the US Central Command said Oct. 11 that US forces disabled the Panama-flagged bulk carrier Akira Sun after it attempted to run the US naval blockade, having previously departed an Iranian port.
Other attacks Oct. 10-11 included strikes on three tankers leaving the Persian Gulf and another tanker near Sohar, Oman,

Nigeria's Dangote refinery extends IPO deadline by two weeks
Refinery delays IPO close date to Oct. 27
Push to relax some upfront payment terms
Offering targets $1.6 billion capital raise
Nigeria's Dangote Petroleum Refinery has extended the closing date for its closely watched IPO by another two weeks after seeking regulatory approval for new payment terms that can help accommodate more international investors, according to an Oct. 12 statement and a company executive.
After launching what is expected to become Africa's largest IPO in history on the Nigerian Stock Exchange in mid-September, the refiner was set to initially close its offer for 4.1 billion ordinary shares by Oct. 13. On the eve of the deadline, however, the company announced it had extended the closing date to Oct. 27, citing demand for extra time from prospective investors.
"The extension is intended to support ongoing market-access initiatives across African jurisdictions, including the expansion of distribution channels," the statement said, citing "very strong interest" from a range of eligible financiers.
In separate comments to Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, a company executive said the group had also sought regulatory support from the Securities and Exchange Commission to soften its requirements for international investors to pay for shares upfront and in full, allowing it to align with the expectations of foreign stakeholders. Under the revised rules, payment will be permitted up to two business days after the allotment date, he said.
"We believe this will materially improve participation by international institutions and enable more efficient capital deployment," the executive said, reiterating the company's focus on stronger pan-African participation in the IPO.
Dangote Petroleum Refinery's public listing is expected to raise more than $1.6 billion for the Nigerian oil refining business, which launched its now 700,000 b/d facility in Lagos in 2024. The company is currently working to double the refinery in size to make it capable of processing 1.4 million b/d of crude, more than any other refinery worldwide, with a target commissioning date in 2029.
Commenting on the deferred closing date for the IPO, refinery CEO David Bird said a two-week extension would allow the company to reach the broadest range of investors and boost participation. The company has aimed to enlist 10 million retail subscribers through the process, a target Bird has previously said it is "well on the way" to meeting.
The company's executive team is in the process of conducting an investor roadshow to drum up African interest in the IPO, with a recent visit to Rwanda and upcoming appearances planned in Botswana, Egypt and Ethiopia. On Sept. 30, it also held a groundbreaking ceremony for a second refinery project in Lamu, Kenya, which it has targeted to bring online by January 2030.

European diesel market doubts Trump's Russian import plan will ease tightnessTraders see promised volumes as relatively small ...from page 1
Russia's diesel/gasoil seaborne exports were 149,000 mt in the week ended Oct. 1, up from 81,000 mt the previous week, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea. This marked the fourth-largest weekly loading since Russia introduced its full ban on diesel and gasoil exports at the beginning of July.
Russia's September exports of 479,000 mt were the lowest monthly diesel/gasoil loadings on record, according to CAS data, which dates back to 2016.
A third European market participant said the effects of the additional diesel volumes could be limited. "It is not a lot of oil, not a lot at all ... these 3 million barrels do not move the needle, other than offering very prompt relief to prices."
While the gasoil spread initially sold off on the news, the market continues to price in a premium for potential supply disruptions.
The ICE low-sulfur gasoil futures October-November spread expired at $35/mt at noon on Oct. 12, down $27.25/mt from the previous settlement, ICE data showed.
Hurricane Isaias has had a bigger impact on diesel than Trump's announcement, with the heating oil-gasoil spread strengthening as a result, the third European market participant said, adding that recent attacks in the Middle East have also raised supply concerns.
S&P Global Energy CERA analysts expect US refinery downtime to average 685,000 b/d in the week ending Oct. 16, primarily due to potential precautionary shutdowns as Hurricane Isaias approaches the northern Gulf Coast, particularly coastal Mississippi and southeastern Louisiana.
Refineries along the Mississippi River corridor could also face indirect disruptions from navigation restrictions and delays in crude and product shipments, according to CERA analysts.
Meanwhile, in a separate announcement Oct. 11, Trump said an energy ceasefire between Russia and Ukraine was "effective immediately."
CERA analysts said that if a lasting energy ceasefire were enacted, it would allow Russia to repair its refineries and increase production/exports, but that would take many months.
-- Sophia Aung

REFINERY NEWS: China's Guangdong Petrochemical prepares for first turnaround
Refinery: Guandong Petrochemical, Jieyang, China
Owner: PetroChina
Overall capacity: 20 million mt/year (around 400,000 b/d)

China's Guangdong Petrochemical prepares for its first turnaround scheduled to begin in late November and last until January 2027, which could temporarily reduce crude runs and refined products output.
The refinery has processed more than 70 million metric tons of crude since its start-up, reaching the milestone at 04:00 Beijing time Sept. 18, as the refinery ran well above the nameplate capacity ahead of its first turnaround, the company said on its official WeChat account late Oct. 8.
The refinery's two crude distillation units, each of 10 million mt/year, have maintained operating rates above 105% for an extended period.
The plant processed 20.06 million mt of crude oil in 2025, reflecting sustained high utilization at the complex, Guangdong Petrochemical said.
Guangdong Petrochemical was fully commissioned on Feb. 27, 2023. The complex comprises 20 million mt/year of refining capacity, 2.6 million mt/year of aromatics production and 1.2 million mt/year of ethylene capacity, according to the company.
-- Staff
REFINERY NEWS: India's HPCL awards EPC contract for Mumbai lube project
Refinery: Mumbai, Maharashtra state, India
Owner: Hindustan Petroleum Corp. Ltd.
Overall capacity: 190,000 b/d
India's state-run refiner Hindustan Petroleum Corp. has awarded a large Engineering, Procurement and Construction (EPC) contract to Technip Energies for executing a lube modernization project at Mumbai refinery, according to a statement by the EPC contractor Oct. 12.
The scope of the project involves lube modernization and bottom upgradation (LMBU) at the Mumbai refinery, the statement said.
The contract covers the delivery of a 550,000 tonnes/annum integrated hydrocracker and catalytic dewaxing (IHCD) unit and an 850,000 metric tons/year solvent deasphalting (SDA) unit, it said.
These units form a major part of HPCL's LMBU project and will be integrated within the operating Mumbai refinery, Technip Energies said in a statement.
"This award by HPCL reflects the confidence placed in our EPC delivery capabilities and builds on a relationship between our two companies spanning more than two decades," said Loïc Chapuis, President Project Delivery and Services at Technip Energies.
The project will modernize HPCL's production of lube oil base stocks, key components used in the manufacture of automotive engine oils and industrial lubricants, the France-based company said.
The project will upgrade heavier refinery streams into highervalue products, improving resource utilization and supporting the refinery's long-term competitiveness, it added.

REFINERY NEWS: Imperial carrying out maintenance at Strathcona plant
Flaring, noise levels rise for 72 hours
Strathcona processes 186,000 barrels daily
Facility produces 20,000 b/d renewable diesel
Imperial Oil is conducting unplanned unit maintenance at its Strathcona refinery in Alberta, the company said in a message posted on the community update line.
The activity could result in greater-than-normal flaring and increased noise levels over the next 72 hours, it said Oct. 11.
The company's message did not include details about the unit undergoing maintenance.
No comments were immediately available from Imperial's media line in Calgary.
The Strathcona refinery accounts for nearly 30% of western Canada's refining capacity, according to information on the Imperial website.
The refinery is located in Strathcona County near Edmonton, Alberta, and supplies the western Canadian and northern US markets with a variety of essential products, including gasoline, diesel, aviation fuel, lubricating oils, petroleum waxes, heavy fuel oil and asphalt, it said.
In 2025, Strathcona refinery processed about 186,000 barrels/ day of crude oil, producing essential products such as gasoline, diesel, aviation fuel, lubricating oils and asphalt, Imperial said. The company also produces 20,000 b/d of renewable diesel at its Strathcona refinery, made by combining hydrogen, Canadiansourced bio-feedstocks such as canola oil, and proprietary technology.

Salavat is not currently processing crude oil after an Oct. 8 attack, according to Russia-based market sources with knowledge of the matter.
Omsk has temporarily halted operations following an Oct. 8 attack but is preparing to restart, according to Russia-based market sources with knowledge of the matter.

Platts invites feedback on US gasoline RVP calendar for 2026 As part of its commitment to open and transparent pricing
and product specifications, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, would like to invite feedback regarding publishing dates and/or cycles for 2026 US gasoline RVP shifts.
The 2026 calendar largely follows 2025 norms, with no notable deviations from prior years. The calendar will be updated throughout the year as more information becomes available.
The shift dates are based on relevant pipeline schedules, historical information, and feedback from market participants.
US Gulf Coast shifts are based on the Colonial Pipeline schedule. US Atlantic Coast shifts are based on Buckeye Pipeline and Colonial Pipeline calendars.
On the West Coast, Los Angeles, San Francisco and Arizona RVP changes are based on Kinder Morgan's SFPP schedule, while Portland and Seattle shifts are based on Olympic Pipeline's calendar.
In the Midwest, Group 3 shifts are based on the ONEOK Pipeline schedule, while Chicago shifts are based on Explorer Pipeline and Buckeye Complex schedules.
The US Environmental Protection Agency removed a waiver in eight Midwestern states that allowed for an additional 1 psi during the summer. Platts will adjust the RVP Calendar for Chicago CBOB in accordance with the implementation of the waiver. More information can be found here.
For questions or comments, please contact americas_products@spglobal.com with a cc to pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00)

plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments during the month of loading.
In view of the changes, Platts is no longer updating the codes reflecting Upper Zakum, Das Blend and Umm Lulu OSP differentials against Murban, as well as the codes for all four grades, including Murban, calculated against the monthly average of Platts cash Dubai 1st Line assessment in the month of trade.
The following codes will no longer be updated from October:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

Diff to Dubai AOSPA00 AOSPB00 AOSPC00 AOSPD00

Diff to Murban OSP
AOSPE00 AOSPF00 AOSPG00

ADNOC's OSPs are published in the Platts price database and on Platts Global Alert page 1051.
Please send all feedback or questions to asia_crude@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if they are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make those not marked as confidential available upon request.

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS

Platts proposes to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of the month, from Dec. 1, 2026.
The proposed change, which follows Platts review of historical data sets, would align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of its ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward value of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.

The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under their price database codes as below:

2-DAY TRAINING
Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.
